- The decision must cover (host, port). A URL decision covers its host and its URL's port (tunnels only under an https decision). A host-level decision (Host or IP) covers its host for the ports of the program's port policy (default 80 and 443; FF_EGRESS_PORTS), narrowed to the ports the tool call declared. Anything else is port_not_allowed. Userinfo, a mismatched Host, un-normalizable hosts and IP literals under a name decision are refused (permission attaches to the name, 4.5).

- Intelligence sources (35.5).

- Kill switches; fail closed when their state is unknown.

- Per-target rate limit: GCRA (14.2, limiter) on key rl:h:<program>:<registrable domain or ip> at the scope document's rate (DefaultRate 10 requests/s, burst 20, concurrency 8 when the document sets none), plus a concurrency cap per key held for the request's or tunnel's lifetime. It applies to every HTTP request, CONNECT tunnel and SOCKS5 tunnel, so a tool's internal parallelism cannot exceed the program rate (inv_10 at the network edge). Denials answer 429 with Retry-After and X-Flowforge-Retry-After-Ms (SOCKS5: reply 2) and count ff_limiter_denied_total{level="proxy_host"|"proxy_host_concurrency"}. With FF_REDIS_ADDR the GCRA state is shared by every replica (Redis TIME as the clock); the concurrency cap is per replica.

- Dial only the decision's pinned addresses, never re-resolving (DNS rebinding cannot redirect a connection); loopback, private, link-local, CGNAT, multicast and unspecified addresses need an explicit cidr rule. Plain-HTTP redirects are re-checked with the scope service.

Every refusal answers 403, 407, 429 or 502 with X-Flowforge-Refused: <reason> (SOCKS5 reply 2, 5 or 7; DNS REFUSED), logs egress.refused and counts ff_egress_refused_total{reason}. Reasons are listed in 40.4.

### 35.3 DNS forwarder

A query is answered only when it carries a valid ticket whose decision is still an allow, no kill switch is engaged, and the name passes a passive scope check (D3; recorded by the scope service; reverse names are checked as the IP they denote) or is an allowlisted intelligence source under a passive decision (recorded as intel_source). Everything else is REFUSED; ANY is NOTIMP; malformed queries get FORMERR. Records of a CNAME chain are kept only up to the first hop that fails the same check; at most 64 records; UDP answers are cut to the client's EDNS size (at most 1232 octets, 512 without EDNS) with TC set (EncodeTruncated), TCP answers to 16 KiB.

### 35.4 Passive tools (decision D3)

DNS resolution (public resolvers or authoritative servers) and TLS handshakes for certificate collection are passive. They still require a scope check: the class is passive, the decision is recorded and IPs are pinned (tlscert asks for pins with resolve = true; without pins the proxy refuses the tunnel, no_pinned_ips). Passive only changes which windows and program classes allow them. portscan and anything that sends application payloads stay active.

### 35.5 Intelligence sources

Passive tools that fetch from public sources (crt.sh, RDAP, Team Cymru) go through the proxy but are checked against an allowlist of intelligence sources, not the program scope: the target is the source, not the program's asset. When the host is on the allowlist and the decision is of class passive, the source's configured or resolved public addresses are dialled and each connection (and each allowlisted DNS name) is recorded in the decision log with reason intel_source; other classes get intel_requires_passive. Redirects must stay on the allowlist. DefaultIntelSources(): crt.sh, data.iana.org, rdap.iana.org, rdap.org, the five RIR RDAP servers, rdap.verisign.com, rdap.publicinterestregistry.org, rdap.identitydigital.services, pubapi.registry.google, rdap.centralnic.com (port 443), whois.cymru.com and asn.cymru.com with subdomains (port 43). FF_EGRESS_INTEL: none (default), default, and/or entries host[:port...][=ip] and *.host.

### 35.6 Notify path

Webhook and Slack deliveries are not program targets. They carry X-Flowforge-Egress-Purpose: notify instead of a decision id; the proxy then requires the service:notifier identity, CONNECT (https only), a destination on FF_NOTIFY_ALLOWLIST (comma-separated names, .example.com for every name below a domain; empty refuses everything), resolves the host itself and connects only when every address passes the 15.4 SSRF check (public unicast), pinned for the connection. The per-target rate limit does not apply (the notifier throttles itself).

## 36. Tools and the language surface

### 36.1 Tools

 | Tool (version) | DSL signature | Class | Emits or returns

 | collect 1.0.0 | (InScope<Url>, String) -> Snapshot (capture profile name) | active | Fetch through the proxy with the profile's request shape; every redirect hop re-checked under its own decision (an out-of-scope hop is not followed: the redirect response is the capture); body up to 8 MiB (longer: truncated, outcome partial); stored with IngestCapture. For an HTML page also its scripts, styles and each script's source map (at most 32, each scope-checked, a denied one skipped and never contacted). Returns the page's Snapshot with the subresources in resources

 | crawl 1.0.0 | (InScope<Url>, Int) -> List<Url> (fetch budget, at most 500) | active | Breadth-first frontier: every URL canonicalized and scope-checked before queueing; robots.txt per origin (2xx parsed, 4xx allow all, 5xx, unreachable or out of scope disallow all; agent token flowforge); links, frames and sitemap entries; revisit policy per (program, URL, profile): adaptive by default (halve on change, double on no change, clamped to [1 h, 7 d], first interval 24 h). Returns every in-scope, robots-allowed URL reached

 | wellknown 1.0.0 | (InScope<Host>) -> List<Url> | active | robots.txt, /.well-known/security.txt then /security.txt, the sitemaps robots.txt names (else /sitemap.xml; an index followed one level, at most 10), OpenAPI at /openapi.json, /swagger.json, /v3/api-docs, /api-docs, /.well-known/openapi.json, GraphQL GET /graphql?query={__typename}. Probes store 2xx answers whose sniffed kind is the expected one

 | extract 1.0.0 | (Snapshot) -> Features | read | The stored features grouped by kind; no graph facts (36.6)

 | unpack_sourcemap 1.0.0 | (Snapshot) -> List<Snapshot> | read | The #src/<path> originals of a stored source map (or of the map a script names)

 | diff_latest 1.0.0 | (Snapshot) -> ChangeSet | read | DiffSnapshots against the stored predecessor (parent, lineage parent, or the baseline); no graph facts (36.6)

 | search_changes 1.0.0 | (String, Int) -> List<ChangeHit> | read | SearchChanges (32.2 query language, limit 1-1000)

 | ctlog 1.0.0 | (Fqdn) -> List<Fqdn> | passive | crt.sh search (intel source): names at or below the input; TLSCertificate (keyed by SHA-256 fingerprint of the downloaded DER, at most 50 per query), common_name / san_dns_name edges

 | rdap 1.0.0 | (Host) -> List<Organization> | passive | IANA bootstrap (cached 24 h) then registry RDAP: DomainRecord, IPNetRecord, Netblock, Organization (never personal data), registration and contains edges

 | asn 1.0.0 | (IpAddress) -> Option<Asn> | passive | RDAP and Team Cymru TXT via the DNS forwarder: AutonomousSystem, Netblock, AutnumRecord, IPNetRecord; announces, contains, registration

 | dnsresolve 2.0.0 | (Fqdn) -> List<IpAddress> | passive | Through the DNS forwarder: A/AAAA, CNAME (every hop scope-checked), MX (PrefDNSRelation), NS, TXT (DNSRecordProperty)

 | portscan 2.0.0 | (InScope<Host>, List<Int>) -> List<Int> | active | Service + port PortRelation per open port, paced per target; tcpconnect 1.1.0 is an alias

 | tlscert 1.0.0 | (InScope<Host>, Int) -> Option<TlsCertificate> | passive (D3) | TLS handshake through a tunnel under a pinned decision, SNI = host, aborted when the certificates arrive (no application data); Service, port, certificate, TLSCertificate, common_name / san_dns_name

 | httpprobe 1.0.0 | (InScope<Host>) -> Option<Service> | active | unchanged from v1

Every URL a content tool touches is checked twice: a passive check first (no resolution, so out-of-scope names never reach DNS), then the active one that resolves and pins. A denied URL is streamed as Skipped and never contacted. A (URL, profile) whose open version the calling run already stored is returned without a fetch, so one run never splits a deploy's change across two versions. new_in_run compares the version's run with the calling run.

### 36.2 Standard-library records

Added to the 7.6 prelude after TaskError (builtin type ids are append-only). Tool results are decoded field by field into these layouts, so adapters emit exactly these fields, in this order.

 | Record | Fields (in order)

 | Snapshot | id: String (snp_...; a derived source-map original is <id>#src/<path>), url: String, profile: String, status: Int, mime: String, kind: String (sniffed), body_sha256: String, raw_size: Int, fetched_at: Time, parent_id: Option<String>, lineage_parent_id: Option<String>, coverage: String, degraded: Bool, new_in_run: Bool, redirects: List<String> (each Location followed, in order), resources: List<Snapshot> (scripts, styles, source maps captured with it)

 | Features | snapshot_id: String, url: String, coverage: String, links, scripts, styles, frames, sourcemaps: List<Url> (canonical), endpoints, forms, parameters (graph keys such as ep:POST https://h/p), headers, libraries, secrets (<rule>:<redacted preview>, never the secret): List<String>

 | ChangeSet | snapshot_id: String, from_snapshot_id: Option<String>, diff_id: Option<String>, url: String, outcome: String, levels: List<String>, semantic: Bool (level 3 present), class: String, newness: String, cause: String, baseline: Bool, alertable: Bool (what default feeds show), score: Float (ranking only), changes: List<String> ("<op> <kind>|<key>"), summary: Map<String, Int> (per-kind counts, plus <kind>_<op>)

 | ChangeHit | score: Float, at: Time, diff_id: String, snapshot_id: String, url: String, kind: String, side: String, feature: String, class: String, newness: String, cause: String, text: String

### 36.3 Builtins added in v2 (G-LANG-10)

Pure builtins over the codec package, appended to the 8.4 builtin table (ids are append-only). Decoders return None for invalid input and for bytes that are not UTF-8, since DSL strings are text.

 | Builtin | Signature | Semantics

 | base64_decode | (s: String) -> Option<String> | RFC 4648 lenient: either alphabet, padding optional, ASCII white space skipped

 | base64_encode | (s: String) -> String | RFC 4648 4 standard alphabet, padded

 | url_decode | (s: String) -> Option<String> | percent-decoding (RFC 3986 2.1), None on a malformed escape; + stays +

 | url_encode | (s: String) -> String | WHATWG component percent-encode set (encodeURIComponent), upper-case hex

 | hex_decode | (s: String) -> Option<String> | base16, either case, white space between pairs skipped

 | hex_encode | (s: String) -> String | lower-case base16 of the UTF-8 bytes

### 36.4 Reference monitoring workflow

conformance/workflows/monitor.ff, monitor(roots: List<Url>, hosts: List<Fqdn>, budget: Int) -> List<String>, runs once per deploy: crawl from every root and wellknown per host; collect every discovered URL with profile default through par_map(..., limit = 4, on_error = "collect"); unpack_sourcemap; diff_latest of every version this run stored (new_in_run, not degraded); keep semantic && alertable; order by score (ranking only) and emit_alert each. Every target passes scope.check before it reaches a tool.

### 36.5 The alert diff variable

Alert rules (15) gain a diff section, present on content events (absent elsewhere; absent values follow the three-valued logic of 15.2):

 | Field | Type | Meaning

 | snapshot_id, from_snapshot_id, diff_id | String | the versions and the stored diff

 | url, profile | String | the capture key

 | outcome, class, newness, cause | String | 33.10

 | levels | List<String> | hash, text, semantic

 | semantic, baseline, alertable | Bool | level 3 present; first complete capture; default-feed visible

 | kinds | List<String> | <kind>_<op>, for example endpoint_added

 | changes | List<String> | <op> <kind>|<key>

 | summary | String | one-line summary (DiffSummary)

 | excerpt | String | redacted unified hunks (34.8)

Example: alert new_endpoint when diff.alertable && "endpoint_added" in diff.kinds -> webhook("w") throttle 1h group_by diff.url. The score and its reasons are deliberately not fields: diff.score is diagnostic A0202 (unknown field). Scores appear in the payload's diff section to rank alerts, but never decide whether one is sent.

### 36.6 One writer per content fact

The content tools stream no observations. The content service publishes each capture's observation (URL, File, Endpoint, ContentProperty, HTTPHeaderProperty, redirects_to, previous_version, ... with coverage-gated authority) in its content.changes event, so every content fact has one writer and authority is applied once. The raw response is the raw snapshot in the tenant's chunk store, not a raw/ blob.

## 37. Content API

### 37.1 ContentService (gRPC, proto/flowforge/v1/content.proto)

 | RPC | Permission | Purpose

 | IngestCapture | runs:submit | Store one capture (idempotent; unchanged re-fetches extend last_seen). Returns the snapshot, diff id, duplicate, unchanged, the change summary (with alertable), the lineage parent URL and the event id

 | GetSnapshot | read; raw needs programs:admin | Metadata; optionally the redacted body, normalized text, features JSON and finding previews; raw restores secrets

 | ListSnapshots | read | Per program, URL, profile; newest first; paged (11.2 paging)

 | DiffSnapshots | read | Unified and/or side-by-side JSON per artifact; from empty means the stored predecessor (or the baseline)

 | SearchChanges, FirstAppeared | read | Section 32 query language over hunks and change records; the earliest snapshot adding a string

 | PinSnapshot, UnpinSnapshot | alerts:write | Retention pins with a reason

 | GetQuotaStatus | read | Limits and usage of 34.6

The tenant comes from the internal token; RLS is set per transaction.

### 37.2 REST (gateway)

 | Route | Parameters | Maps to

 | GET /v1/snapshots | program_id (required), url, profile, open_only, paging | ListSnapshots

 | GET /v1/snapshots/{id} | include=body,normalized,features, raw=true | GetSnapshot

 | GET /v1/snapshots/{a}/diff/{b} | format=unified|side_by_side|both (default both); {a} may be previous | DiffSnapshots

 | POST /v1/snapshots/{id}/:pin, POST /v1/snapshots/{id}/:unpin | body {"reason": "..."} for pin | PinSnapshot, UnpinSnapshot

 | GET /v1/changes/search | program_id, q, limit (1-1000), include_vendor | SearchChanges

 | GET /v1/changes/first-appeared | program_id, text, include_vendor | FirstAppeared

 | GET /v1/content/quota |  | GetQuotaStatus

Snapshot ids carry the prefix snp_. JSON field names are the proto field names (snake case); optional strings are omitted when empty. A snapshot: id, program_id, entity_id, url, url_template, profile, run_id, fetched_at, last_seen, valid_from, valid_to, status, mime, kind, body_sha256, raw_size, stored_size, normalizer_version, parent_id, lineage_parent_id, outcome, coverage, degraded, pinned, pin_reason, created_at, plus body, body_raw, normalized, headers, features, findings (rule_id, preview, keyed_hash) when included. A change summary: outcome, levels, class, newness, cause, baseline, score, score_features, changes (op, kind, key, newness, value), summary. A diff artifact: doc_id, kind, outcome, levels, algorithm, bound_level, fallback, inserted, deleted, unified, side_by_side, feature_changes. A hit: score, at, diff_id, snapshot_id, from_snapshot_id, url, doc, header, kind, side, feature, mime, url_template, class, newness, cause, vendor, text, decoded_from.

Errors follow 12.2. Content reasons: NOT_FOUND (404), VALIDATION_FAILED (400; query errors carry the textindex code and span of 40.3 in the error metadata), QUOTA_EXCEEDED (429; metadata quota = captures_per_day | stored_bytes | index_bytes, Retry-After for the daily quota), CAPTURE_OUT_OF_ORDER (409), VERSION_CONFLICT (retried internally), CONTENT_INTEGRITY (500: stored bytes failed verification), INTERNAL.

## 38. Lab and end-to-end acceptance

An in-process lab (services/internal/lab) is a small deterministic internet: an in-scope TLS listener on 127.0.0.1 with virtual hosts and one Ed25519 certificate signed by the lab CA, an in-scope plain-HTTP listener, an out-of-scope listener on 127.0.0.2 (Linux loopback /8), an authoritative DNS server (A records for the lab hosts, NODATA for other types, NXDOMAIN inside the lab zones, REFUSED outside), a clock that jumps to each deploy's time, and logs of every request and DNS question. Volatile values ({{nonce}}, {{ts}}, {{ts_s}}, {{iso}}, {{request_id}}) come from SplitMix64 with the manifest's seed.

A site is conformance/lab/<site>/manifest.json: hosts with scope in or out; deploys (files added, replaced or removed per listener, host and path; redirects); and expect: the planted level-3 changes (deploy, snapshot URL, <op> <kind>|<key>, a regex that finds it), the renamed bundles and their predecessors, notifier rules with alert counts, and robots-disallowed paths. The acceptance site shop has six weekly deploys: d1 baseline; d2 only volatile values, formatting, build hashes and a reordered, renamed bundle; d3 a new POST form, a third-party script without SRI, a leaked key (the AWS documentation example pair), CSP loosened, X-Frame-Options removed, HttpOnly dropped, renamed chunks with new admin endpoints and a library bump; d4 CSP removed, a DELETE endpoint in a renamed bundle and in the OpenAPI document; d5 and d6 only per-response volatile values.

TestLabAcceptance_Memory and TestLabAcceptance_Postgres start the lab and the dev stack (engine, tool runner, egress proxy, scope, content, graph, notifier), create a program allowing the in-scope hosts, register monitor.ff and run it once per deploy. Each item MUST hold:

 | Item | Assertion | Measured (2026-11-13)

 | 1 capture each deploy | every page, script, style and map the deploy serves was requested in that deploy and has a stored version valid at its time | all 6 deploys

 | 2 exactly the planted level-3 changes | the level-3 changes of all stored versions equal the planted ones, each alertable; no text-only (structural) change; each run's report lists exactly its deploy's changed versions | 18 = 18 over 49 versions

 | 3 renamed chunks linked | each renamed bundle's first version has its predecessor as lineage parent, and the graph has File → File previous_version | 5 renames

 | 4 alerts once each | per rule exactly the planted count; the webhook received each once with a valid signature; content alerts carry diff; none carries the key | 19 deliveries

 | 5 searchable | each planted change found at its version by search_changes, by kind:<kind>_<op> and by the manifest's regex | 18 × 2 queries

 | 6 no out-of-scope contact | no request on the out-of-scope listener or for an out-of-scope Host, no DNS question for an out-of-scope name, no robots-disallowed path, refusals recorded for both out-of-scope hosts | 254 requests and 538 DNS questions, 0 out of scope

 | 7 storage under 25 % of raw | raw snapshots (capture record + redacted body of every version, CSTORE-INV-08's measure) against the versions' raw size | 6.8 % (all records with diffs 18.4 %)

Run: go test ./services/internal/lab/ (memory) and FF_TEST_PG_DSN=... go test ./services/internal/lab/ -run Postgres.

## 39. Invariant register

Every v2 package's numbered invariants, each with its test (Test<Pkg>Inv<NN>_<Name>, name-scanned by verify-module through the required ids in modules.toml) and its one-line statement. The Rust port reimplements exactly this list under the same ids (snake case: html_inv_01_token_spans_tile_input). Fuzz targets assert the same invariants on arbitrary input. The list is generated from the package READMEs, which MUST stay in step with this table; every test named here exists.

#### htmlparse

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | HTML-INV-01 | TestHTMLInv01_TokenSpansTileInput | Token spans start at 0, are contiguous and the EOF token ends at len(input), in every initial state.

 | HTML-INV-02 | TestHTMLInv02_ParsingIsTotal | Tokenize, Parse and ParseFragment accept any bytes without panicking; Parse always yields an html document element.

 | HTML-INV-03 | TestHTMLInv03_SerializeReparseFixpoint | For round-trip-safe trees (roundTripSafe: well-formed generated documents and the safe subset of soup), Parse(Render(Parse(x))) dumps like Parse(x).

 | HTML-INV-04 | TestHTMLInv04_LinearTimeAndAllocation | Allocation count and bytes per input byte at 4n are within 20 % of n; time per byte within 50 % (skipped under -race/-short).

 | HTML-INV-05 | TestHTMLInv05_ArenaIntegrity | All links are in range and mutually consistent, sibling chains are acyclic, every node is reached exactly once from a parentless node.

 | HTML-INV-06 | TestHTMLInv06_DepthLimit | The stack of open elements never exceeds MaxDepth; exceeding it reports the error, keeps every element and keeps parsing.

 | HTML-INV-07 | TestHTMLInv07_DecodeTokenizeEquivalence | Valid UTF-8 decodes to itself with or without a BOM or utf-8 label, and tokenizes identically.

 | HTML-INV-08 | TestHTMLInv08_ErrorsOrderedAndLocated | Errors are sorted by offset, lie within the input, and Line/Col (UTF-16 columns, CR LF = one break) match the offset.

 | HTML-INV-09 | TestHTMLInv09_AttributesUniqueAndLimited | No element has duplicate (namespace, name) attributes; MaxAttributes caps each tag with an error.

 | HTML-INV-10 | TestHTMLInv10_TextCoalesced | The parser never leaves two adjacent Text siblings.

 | HTML-INV-11 | TestHTMLInv11_NamedReferenceLongestMatch | Both lookup strategies return the brute-force longest entity prefix; entities.json equals the table.

#### jsparse

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | JS-INV-01 | TestJSInv01_TokenTiling | With Options.Tokens, tokens and trivia tile the input exactly once, in order, for every valid input and, in tolerant mode, for every input.

 | JS-INV-02 | TestJSInv02_Totality, FuzzJSParse | Tolerant mode returns a program for any byte string and never panics; strict mode returns an error or a program; diagnostics have known codes and in-range spans.

 | JS-INV-03 | TestJSInv03_PrintReparse, TestJSInv03_PrintReparseGenerated, FuzzJSPrintReparse | Parse(Print(p)) has the same canonical JSON as p, in readable and minified layouts (all pass files, generated programs, and the whole positive test262 corpus in TestJSTest262Full).

 | JS-INV-04 | TestJSInv04_LinearTime | Parsing a 4n-byte bundle costs at most 1.6 × 4 × the time and allocated bytes of an n-byte bundle (best of five).

 | JS-INV-05 | TestJSInv05_LexicalGoals | Every /-initial token is a RegularExpressionLiteral exactly where the grammar admits one (hand-written ambiguous contexts and the generator's recorded answers); regex tokens are lexed under a RegExp goal.

 | JS-INV-06 | TestJSInv06_ArenaIntegrity | Child ids are in range and not self; list ranges are inside Children; holes only in array elements; no node has two parents; child spans lie within parent spans; in successful parses every node is reachable from the root.

 | JS-INV-07 | TestJSInv07_TolerantAgrees | On valid input tolerant mode gives the strict tree and no diagnostics.

 | JS-INV-08 | TestJSInv08_FirstDiagnosticMatchesStrict | On invalid input the first tolerant diagnostic equals the strict-mode error.

 | JS-INV-09 | TestJSInv09_CanonicalJSON | The canonical JSON is valid JSON with strictly sorted object keys and is identical across parses.

 | JS-INV-10 | TestJSInv10_LexerTiling, FuzzJSLexer | The lexer alone, under any goal sequence, terminates and tiles the input with tokens and trivia.

#### cssparse

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | CSS-INV-01 | TestCSSInv01_TokenTiling | Token spans are non-empty, contiguous, and cover the input exactly once.

 | CSS-INV-02 | TestCSSInv02_Totality | Every entry point accepts any byte string and yields a well-formed arena (parent/child links, ordered in-range spans); URL extraction never fails.

 | CSS-INV-03 | TestCSSInv03_Reserialization | Concatenating the token source spans reproduces the input byte for byte; Serialize (from values) re-tokenizes to the same kinds, flags, values and numbers.

 | CSS-INV-04 | TestCSSInv04_LinearSteps, TestCSSInv04_LinearTime | Tokenizer state-machine steps ≤ 4·n + 8; nodes ≤ (MaxDepth+2)·tokens; allocations and time at 4n within 4.4x / 1.5x of n.

 | CSS-INV-05 | TestCSSInv05_LosslessComponentTree | In a component-value parse without a depth hit, every non-comment token is exactly one node's token or one block's closing token.

 | CSS-INV-06 | TestCSSInv06_DepthBound | No block or function is nested deeper than MaxDepth (re-parsed blocks included); depth_limit is reported exactly when the true nesting exceeds it.

 | CSS-INV-07 | TestCSSInv07_EveryURLTokenReported | Every <url-token> of a stylesheet appears in URLs() with its span, in source order.

 | CSS-INV-08 | TestCSSInv08_LimitsGradePartial | A result is partial exactly when a limit was hit, never complete then.

#### xmlparse

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | XML-INV-01 | TestXMLInv01_Totality | Any byte string yields a tree and nil or a *SyntaxError with an in-range offset; EOF-class errors sit at the input length; the grade matches the error.

 | XML-INV-02 | TestXMLInv02_FirstOffendingByte | Every prefix of a well-formed document is error-free or ends in an EOF-class error; for an error at k, [:k] has no non-EOF error and [:k+w] (alone or followed by anything) gives the same code at k.

 | XML-INV-03 | TestXMLInv03_DepthCap | Nesting beyond MaxDepth stops with depth_limit (partial) and no tree path exceeds MaxDepth.

 | XML-INV-04 | TestXMLInv04_CanonicalRoundTrip | Generated documents (random escaping, quoting, CDATA, whitespace, namespaces) parse to the model's canonical form, and canonical form is a fixed point of parse-then-canonicalize.

 | XML-INV-05 | TestXMLInv05_NoExpansionGrowth | Decoded text, names and attribute values never exceed the input length; any internal subset is refused (unsupported).

 | XML-INV-06 | TestXMLInv06_LinearSteps, TestXMLInv06_LinearTime | Decoder steps ≤ 3·n + 16 on generated and adversarial input; tree allocations at 4n ≤ 4.4x of n; decoder time per byte at 4n ≤ 1.5x of n.

 | XML-INV-07 | TestXMLInv07_LimitsGradePartial | A result is partial exactly when a limit stopped it, never complete then.

 | XML-INV-08 | TestXMLInv08_EventsNestAndStick | Events nest with matching names, the tree has one node per content event, and the error is sticky.

 | SITEMAP-INV-01 | TestSitemapInv01_ExactEntries | Generated sitemaps (plain or gzip, with invalid entries and look-alike <loc> in extensions) yield exactly the valid entries in order.

 | SITEMAP-INV-02 | TestSitemapInv02_LimitsGradePartial | Entry/byte limits grade the read partial, entries never exceed the limit, decompression never inflates past MaxBytes+1.

#### sourcemap

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | SMAP-INV-01 | TestSMapInv01_VLQRoundTrip | DecodeVLQ(AppendVLQ(v)) = v for random and boundary int32 values (MinInt32 included); encodings are minimal and at most 7 digits.

 | SMAP-INV-02 | TestSMapInv02_MappingsRoundTrip | decode(encode(ms)) = ms (in-line order kept, columns to MaxInt32) and encode(decode(s)) = s for canonical s.

 | SMAP-INV-03 | TestSMapInv03_LookupMatchesBruteForce | OriginalPositionFor agrees with a linear scan.

 | SMAP-INV-04 | TestSMapInv04_SortedAndInBounds | Every accepted map (regular, index, mutated) has mappings sorted by generated position with every index and position in range.

 | SMAP-INV-05 | TestSMapInv05_IndexMapAgreesWithSections | Lookup in a flattened index map equals lookup in the section's own map, offset-adjusted.

 | SMAP-INV-06 | TestSMapInv06_LimitsGradePartial | A limit hit is partial, never complete; partial maps keep SMAP-INV-04.

 | SMAP-INV-07 | TestSMapInv07_LinearTime | Allocations at 4n ≤ 4.4x of n; time per byte ≤ 1.5x.

#### wellknown

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | WK-INV-01 | TestWKInv01_RobotsMatchesReference | On generated robots.txt files, Allowed agrees with a brute-force reference implementation of RFC 9309 semantics for random crawlers and paths.

 | WK-INV-02 | TestWKInv02_PatternMatcherMatchesBruteForce | The backtracking pattern matcher equals exhaustive wildcard matching.

 | WK-INV-03 | TestWKInv03_Totality | Any byte string parses as both formats; diagnostics carry in-range line numbers; every group has an agent.

 | WK-INV-04 | TestWKInv04_LimitsGradePartial | Byte and item limits grade both formats partial, never complete, and no more items than the limit are kept.

 | WK-INV-05 | TestWKInv05_ExpiresAgainstArgument | expired holds exactly when Expires ≤ now; results are deterministic for the same input and time.

 | WK-INV-06 | TestWKInv06_SignedFramingTransparent | Wrapping a file in a cleartext signature (with dash-escaping) gives the same fields and diagnostics, with Signed set.

 | WK-INV-07 | TestWKInv07_LinearParse | robots.txt parsing allocations at 4n ≤ 4.4x of n; time per byte ≤ 1.5x.

#### codec

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | CODEC-INV-01 | TestCodecInv01_RoundTrip | decode(encode(x)) == x in both modes for every codec and variant (JS over UTF-8/WTF-8, forms over UTF-8 pairs), and Inflate inverts the reference gzip, zlib, DEFLATE and zstd encoders.

 | CODEC-INV-02 | TestCodecInv02_StrictCanonical | For base64, base32, hex, percent (every set), form components and forms, strict decoding accepts s iff s == encode(decode(s)) (base32 and hex up to ASCII case).

 | CODEC-INV-03 | TestCodecInv03_StrictConformsSpec | Quoted-printable and JS strict modes accept every encoder output (QP lines ≤ 76) and reject each planted violation of RFC 2045 §6.7 / ECMA-262 strict mode with its code and offset; lenient QP and JS decoding are total.

 | CODEC-INV-04 | TestCodecInv04_LenientSuperset | Whatever strict accepts, lenient accepts with the same output.

 | CODEC-INV-05 | TestCodecInv05_StdlibAgreement | Differential against encoding/base64 (strict both ways, lenient ⊇ stdlib), encoding/base32 (lenient ⊇), encoding/hex (equal), net/url (PathUnescape equal to the RFC 3986 check, QueryUnescape/ParseQuery ⊆ form, both invert our encoders), mime/quotedprintable (lenient ⊇; it inverts EncodeQP; strict accepts its binary writer), encoding/json string escapes, and compress/* + klauspost zstd on intact and corrupted streams.

 | CODEC-INV-06 | TestCodecInv06_Base64AlgosAgree | Every Base64Algo candidate returns identical bytes, or the identical error code and offset, for every option set and input.

 | CODEC-INV-07 | TestCodecInv07_OutputLimits | Inflate never returns more than its cap; at the cap it returns exactly that prefix with ErrOutputLimit (bombs: 12 MiB of zeros through every format, a 1 GiB zstd RLE frame); below it the output is complete. Text decoders never output more bytes than they read.

 | CODEC-INV-08 | TestCodecInv08_LayersBounded | DecodeLayers stays within MaxDepth, MaxTotal and MaxNodes with a consistent arena; a payload wrapped k ≤ MaxDepth times (base64, base64url, hex, gzip, zlib, zstd in random order) is found as text at depth k with that path; a gzip-in-gzip bomb stays within the total.

 | CODEC-INV-09 | TestCodecInv09_SniffSound | Sniff returns exactly the planted base64/hex blobs in filler text with exact spans; on any input candidates are sorted, disjoint, in bounds and decode with their kind.

 | CODEC-INV-10 | TestCodecInv10_Determinism | Same input, same bytes: encoders, decoders, Sniff, DecodeLayers; Append* appends exactly what the plain function returns, whatever dst holds.

 | CODEC-INV-11 | TestCodecInv11_StableErrors | Every error is an *Error with a code from the closed list unwrapping to its sentinel, the offset is within the input (text) or equals the returned length (streams), and failing text decoders leave dst unchanged.

#### dnsmsg

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | DNS-INV-01 | TestDNSInv01_RoundTrip | Decode(Encode(m)) == m for generated canonical messages, both modes, no diagnostics

 | DNS-INV-02 | TestDNSInv02_PointersTerminate | Compression pointers never loop: self, forward, backward-cyclic and over-long chains end in pointer_loop; pointer-dense random input terminates

 | DNS-INV-03 | TestDNSInv03_NameBounds | Every decoded name is at most 255 octets in wire form with labels of at most 63, and is canonical

 | DNS-INV-04 | TestDNSInv04_Totality | Decoding is total: any bytes give a message or an error / diagnostics from the closed list

 | DNS-INV-05 | TestDNSInv05_CanonicalEncode | Encoding is canonical: equal messages give equal bytes, and decode-then-encode reproduces them

 | DNS-INV-06 | TestDNSInv06_StrictIdempotence | Strict idempotence: whatever decodes strictly re-encodes to bytes that decode to the same message

 | DNS-INV-07 | TestDNSInv07_Truncation | EncodeTruncated fits the limit, sets TC exactly when records were dropped, keeps a prefix of the answers and the OPT record, and decodes strictly

 | DNS-INV-08 | TestDNSInv08_TCPFraming | TCP framing round-trips and refuses frames above the limit

#### socks5

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | SOCKS-INV-01 | TestSOCKSInv01_RoundTrip | Every message round-trips: parse(append(m)) uses exactly its octets and re-encodes identically

 | SOCKS-INV-02 | TestSOCKSInv02_Totality | Parsing is total: a value whose canonical encoding is the consumed prefix, or a closed-list error

 | SOCKS-INV-03 | TestSOCKSInv03_Prefix | Every strict prefix of a valid message is short with len(prefix) < Need <= len(message)

 | SOCKS-INV-04 | TestSOCKSInv04_UnsupportedRefused | Unsupported commands, address types, methods and credentials are refused with the RFC reply; nothing but CONNECT reaches the caller

#### diff

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | DIFF-INV-01 | TestDiffInv01_ApplyCorrect | Every algorithm returns a canonical script and Apply(a, Diff(a, b)) == b

 | DIFF-INV-02 | TestDiffInv02_MyersMinimal | myers-linear and myers distances equal the reference DP's n+m−2·LCS

 | DIFF-INV-03 | TestDiffInv03_HeuristicsBoundedByMyers | patience and histogram are Apply-correct with distance ≥ the Myers distance

 | DIFF-INV-04 | TestDiffInv04_Invert | Apply(b, Invert(s)) == a; Invert is an involution preserving distance

 | DIFF-INV-05 | TestDiffInv05_Compose | Compose(s1, s2) is canonical, applies a→c, distance ≤ d1+d2, identity is neutral, mismatched scripts are rejected

 | DIFF-INV-06 | TestDiffInv06_Hunks | Hunks cover every change exactly once, in order; context is exactly min(C, available); hunks are separated by > 2C equal tokens, so contexts never overlap or touch

 | DIFF-INV-07 | TestDiffInv07_UnifiedRoundTrip | Unified → ParseUnified → ApplyUnified(old) == new for every algorithm and context; parsed headers equal HunkHeader; a tampered base is refused

 | DIFF-INV-08 | TestDiffInv08_GitCompatibility (unified_git.json) | Git compatibility: with GitCompact, Unified reproduces git 2.43's hunks byte for byte (histogram always; patience without Myers fallback; Myers where git agrees)

 | DIFF-INV-09 | TestDiffInv09_JSONPatchRoundTrip | ApplyPatch(a, JSONDiff(a, b)) is Equal to b; equal documents give an empty patch; patch and document encodings round trip

 | DIFF-INV-10 | TestDiffInv10_JSONEquality | Equal is RFC 6902 equality (reflexive, symmetric, order-insensitive objects, numeric numbers) and agrees with hash-consed ids

 | DIFF-INV-11 | TestDiffInv11_TreeMetric | Tree edit distance is a metric on random trees (identity, symmetry, triangle) and its mapping realises it

 | DIFF-INV-12 | TestDiffInv12_TreeBruteForce | Zhang–Shasha equals an exhaustive Tai-mapping search on tiny trees (unit and other costs); the mapping is a valid Tai mapping costing the distance

 | DIFF-INV-13 | TestDiffInv13_Determinism | Determinism: identical inputs give byte-identical scripts, unified text, side-by-side JSON, patches and tree mappings across runs

 | DIFF-INV-14 | TestDiffInv14_LinearSpaceMemory | Linear-space Myers allocates O(N+M) (≤ 96 B/token, ≤ 5× bytes for 4× input) while the greedy trace grows quadratically

 | DIFF-INV-15 | TestDiffInv15_CapsAndFallbacks | A cap below D yields Capped with an Apply-correct script keeping the common prefix; a cap ≥ D changes nothing; BoundedDiff (any pluggable algorithms) reports level and reason and its script applies at that level

 | DIFF-INV-16 | TestDiffInv16_SemanticCleanup | CleanupSemantic is Apply-correct, canonical, never below minimal, leaves no eliminable equality (except step-4 overlaps) and is idempotent

 | DIFF-INV-17 | TestDiffInv17_WordRefinement | Word refinement spans are sorted, disjoint, in bounds; removing them leaves equal text

 | DIFF-INV-18 | TestDiffInv18_SideBySide | Side-by-side rows reconstruct each hunk's old and new lines with consecutive line numbers

 | DIFF-INV-19 | TestDiffInv19_SplitAndIntern | SplitLines/SplitWords tile the input; interned ids are dense, first-seen, equal iff strings are equal

 | DIFF-INV-20 | TestDiffInv20_ReferenceExhaustive | reference equals exhaustive LCS recursion on tiny inputs and refuses inputs over MaxReferenceCells

#### sketch

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | SKETCH-INV-01 | TestSketchInv01_VecindexCompat, TestSketchConformance* | Outputs equal the pre-refactor vecindex outputs (conformance/sketch/vecindex_compat.json, conformance/vecindex/{simhash,minhash})

 | SKETCH-INV-02 | TestSketchInv02_SimHashTracksJaccard | SimHash Hamming distance tracks shingle Jaccard: per bucket the mean distance is within 2.5 bits of Charikar's 64·θ/π and strictly increases as Jaccard falls

 | SKETCH-INV-03 | TestSketchInv03_MinHashEstimate | MinHash estimate within 5 standard errors of exact Jaccard for every pair; mean error < 0.01

 | SKETCH-INV-04 | TestSketchInv04_LSHCollisionRate | LSH collision rate matches 1−(1−s^r)^b within 5 binomial standard errors (≥ 0.05)

 | SKETCH-INV-05 | TestSketchInv05_WeightedConsistency | Weighted SimHash with unit weights = SimHash; term-frequency weights = multiset SimHash; positive scaling is invariant

 | SKETCH-INV-06 | TestSketchInv06_ShinglingExact | WShingling is exactly the set of w-windows (size and Jaccard); TokenShingles has max(n−k+1, min(n,1)) elements

 | SKETCH-INV-07 | TestSketchInv07_SimHashIndexExact | SimHashIndex.Near = brute-force scan for distance ≤ 3 after any adds/removes

 | SKETCH-INV-08 | TestSketchInv08_LSHCandidatesExact | LSH.Candidates = brute-force "agrees on a whole band"

 | SKETCH-INV-09 | TestSketchInv09_OrderIndependence | SimHash ignores order; MinHash ignores order and duplicates; SignatureHashes(FNV1a64(e)) = Signature(e)

 | SKETCH-INV-10 | TestSketchInv10_MulAddMod61 | MulAddMod61 = schoolbook 128-bit reference

#### chunkstore

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | CHUNK-INV-01 | TestChunkInv01_RoundTrip | Get(Put(x)) == x for sizes 0, 1, every min/avg/max boundary ±1 and random sizes, in several configurations; Stat reports the size.

 | CHUNK-INV-02 | TestChunkInv02_BoundariesArePure | Cut points are a pure function of the bytes since the previous cut: re-chunking from any cut reproduces the remaining cuts.

 | CHUNK-INV-03 | TestChunkInv03_ShiftResistance | Inserting 1–64 bytes in the first 4 KiB of 1 MiB of random data preserves ≥ 90 % of the chunks (and changes at most a constant number).

 | CHUNK-INV-04 | TestChunkInv04_ChunkLengthBounds | Every chunk is in [min, max] except the last, which is in [1, max]; cuts increase strictly and end at the input length.

 | CHUNK-INV-05 | TestChunkInv05_StreamingEqualsSlice | The streaming chunker yields the slice API's boundaries for every read size (1 B … 1 MiB, one-byte, half, data+EOF, random).

 | CHUNK-INV-06 | TestChunkInv06_Dedupe | Storing the same content twice adds zero chunks and zero bytes and writes nothing; repeats inside one input are stored once.

 | CHUNK-INV-07 | TestChunkInv07_StoreCorruptionDetected | Flipping any byte of any pack, index or ref is reported by fsck as that object's *_BAD_MAGIC (bytes 0–3) or *_CHECKSUM code; Open fails with it for indexes and refs; Get never returns wrong bytes.

 | CHUNK-INV-08 | TestChunkInv08_DecoderCorruptionCodes | Each decoder maps any single flipped byte to *_BAD_MAGIC/*_CHECKSUM and any truncation to a coded error; a flipped record byte gives RECORD_MALFORMED (lengths) or RECORD_CHECKSUM.

 | CHUNK-INV-09 | TestChunkInv09_GCSafetyAndReclaim | Over random put/drop/pin/GC/reopen sequences, GC never removes a chunk reachable from a live, pinned or grace manifest; fsck is clean; no pack stays below the live threshold; threshold 100 leaves nothing unreachable.

 | CHUNK-INV-10 | TestChunkInv10_GCCrashSafety | A crash injected at every mutating backend call of GC (call fails, or succeeds with the ack lost) leaves a consistent, complete store; the next GC finishes the job with a problem-free fsck.

 | CHUNK-INV-11 | TestChunkInv11_Determinism | Same inputs, order and clock → byte-identical packs, indexes, manifests and ref; manifest ids do not depend on packing; index bytes do not depend on entry order.

 | CHUNK-INV-12 | TestChunkInv12_ZstdDeterminismAndFormatIndependence | The pinned encoder is byte-deterministic (instances, repeats, concurrency 1 vs 8); records written by other encoders/levels read back with the same ids and a clean fsck.

 | CHUNK-INV-13 | TestChunkInv13_ConformanceBoundaries | The gear table and the cut points/chunk hashes of the seeded inputs equal gear.json and boundaries.json; the default masks are the paper's.

 | CHUNK-INV-14 | TestChunkInv14_GoldenFormats | The golden pack, index, manifest and ref decode to their documented fields, and encoding those fields reproduces the bytes.

 | CHUNK-INV-15 | TestChunkInv15_PutCrashSafety | A crash at any mutating call of a Put leaves an fsck-clean store with every earlier blob intact; the interrupted blob is complete or absent.

 | CHUNK-INV-16 | TestChunkInv16_Concurrency | Concurrent Put/Get/GC are race-free and lose no blob whose Put completed.

 | CHUNK-INV-17 | TestChunkInv17_TenantIsolation | Two namespaces with identical content each pay for their own chunks; presence, Get, Stat, Stats, Metrics, GC and Fsck of one never reflect the other; namespaces are mandatory and validated.

 | CHUNK-INV-18 | TestChunkInv18_Pinning | Pinned manifests survive every GC regardless of the live set; an unknown pinned id aborts GC with nothing changed.

 | CHUNK-INV-19 | TestChunkInv19_IndexLookupMatchesScan | Fan-out + binary-search lookup equals a linear scan for present and absent ids.

 | CHUNK-INV-20 | TestChunkInv20_ReopenPersistence | A reopened store serves the same blobs and dedupes against them.

 | CHUNK-INV-21 | TestChunkInv21_GCGrace | A blob put before a GC survives that GC unnamed and is collected by the next GC that does not name it.

 | CHUNK-INV-22 | TestChunkInv22_RecompressionRule | GC recompression keeps ids and content, stores zstd only when 8*stored_len <= 7*raw_len and never grows a record.

 | CHUNK-INV-23 | TestChunkInv23_MetricsConsistency | Metrics equal the sums of Put stats and the backend's object sizes; ratios follow from the counters.

#### textindex

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | TIDX-INV-01 | TestTIdxInv01_SearchMatchesBruteForce | Hits, scores (bit-exact), order, totals and error codes equal ScanIndex for random corpora (up to 1,500 docs, skip blocks included) and random queries (terms, phrases, regexes, filters, negation, nesting).

 | TIDX-INV-02 | TestTIdxInv02_OpsMatchBruteForce | The same after random interleavings of add, update, delete, refresh, merge, force-merge and reopen; Get agrees.

 | TIDX-INV-03 | TestTIdxInv03_RegexPrefilterNeverDrops | If a regexp matches a text, the text's trigrams satisfy its trigram query; index regex totals equal a brute-force regexp scan.

 | TIDX-INV-04 | TestTIdxInv04_BM25MatchesTextbook | Single-term scores equal an independent textbook BM25 over live docs (1e-12 relative); BM25 is increasing and saturating in tf.

 | TIDX-INV-05 | TestTIdxInv05_MergePreservesResults | Policy and forced merges change no result; a full merge leaves no tombstones.

 | TIDX-INV-06 | TestTIdxInv06_SegmentRoundTrip | encode → open → decode is the identity on normalized documents and generations, for every codec; every analyzed term is in the dictionary.

 | TIDX-INV-07 | TestTIdxInv07_CorruptionDetected | Every single-byte change (three masks) and truncation of a segment (every codec), tombstone file or commit point is rejected.

 | TIDX-INV-08 | TestTIdxInv08_SegmentDeterminism | Segment bytes do not depend on input order; merging any split of a document set gives the bytes of encoding it directly.

 | TIDX-INV-09 | TestTIdxInv09_CompoundTokensRetrievable | Searching for any emitted compound or word token finds its document.

 | TIDX-INV-10 | TestTIdxInv10_AnalyzerTokenInvariants | Token spans are in bounds on rune boundaries, Term = fold(span), positions are non-decreasing and below the field length, parts strictly increase and contain no delimiter, a compound shares its first part's position.

 | TIDX-INV-11 | TestTIdxInv11_CaseInsensitive | Folding is idempotent and equal for all case variants of every rune; random case variants of a document's words find it.

 | TIDX-INV-12 | TestTIdxInv12_PrintParseIdentity | Parse(Print(Parse(q))) equals Parse(q) up to spans and printing is a fixpoint.

 | TIDX-INV-13 | TestTIdxInv13_ErrorSpansInsideQuery | Every parse, lowering and execution error is a *QueryError with a listed code and a span inside the query.

 | TIDX-INV-14 | TestTIdxInv14_VisibilityAndDurability | Writes are invisible until Refresh; reopening a memory or file-system directory reproduces every result.

 | TIDX-INV-15 | TestTIdxInv15_SkipsAgreeWithScan | Postings advance over skip entries equals a linear scan; leapfrog intersection equals set intersection.

 | TIDX-INV-16 | TestTIdxInv16_RegexWithoutTrigramsRule | A trigram-less regex is rejected unless the root filters select at most the cap; then it is answered exactly.

 | TIDX-INV-17 | TestTIdxInv17_RRFMatchesVecindex | RRF reproduces conformance/vecindex/rrf (constant 60, 1-based ranks, first occurrence, ties by id).

 | TIDX-INV-18 | TestTIdxInv18_AlgorithmsInterchangeable | Every codec × intersector × regex strategy equals ScanIndex; with TF-IDF both engines still agree.

#### content

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | CONTENT-INV-01 | TestContentInv01_NormalizeIdempotent | For every kind, Normalize(k, Normalize(k, x).Text).Text == Normalize(k, x).Text on fixtures and generated, often malformed, documents, with and without learned rules.

 | CONTENT-INV-02 | TestContentInv02_VolatileInvariantSemanticDetected | Normalization is invariant under the declared volatile transformations (nonce, CSRF, timestamps, cache-bust, build hashes, mark-up and minifier re-formatting); a planted semantic change (link, script, form, endpoint, frame) is always detected as a feature.

 | CONTENT-INV-03 | TestContentInv03_MasksNeverHideFeatures | Learned rules never change features; rules on level-3 paths are refused by Validate and never frozen by LearnVolatility (both detectors); path-like values are never masked.

 | CONTENT-INV-04 | TestContentInv04_HiddenFieldsRedacted | Hidden form fields keep only short numeric or boolean values in normalized output.

 | CONTENT-INV-05 | TestContentInv05_IdenticalDiffEmpty | Diffing identical captures under every diff policy gives no levels, no hunks, no feature changes, outcome no_change, no class, score 0.

 | CONTENT-INV-06 | TestContentInv06_DegradedNeverComplete | Login redirect, challenge, CAPTCHA with a blocking status, error after success, soft 404, mass removal, catch-all, truncation, fetch failure and a partial collector outcome never give coverage complete; their observations never withdraw; degraded captures get no diff.

 | CONTENT-INV-07 | TestContentInv07_PartialGradeNotComplete | Any extractor grade below complete makes coverage not complete and leaves that extractor's families uncovered.

 | CONTENT-INV-08 | TestContentInv08_LineageRenamedMatched | Every lineage matcher links a renamed chunk with a few edits and renamed locals to its predecessor and never to an unrelated bundle; a unique lineage key decides first.

 | CONTENT-INV-09 | TestContentInv09_ModuleMovesNoChange | Reordering bundler modules and renaming their locals yields no module change and no semantic change; editing one module yields exactly one changed module.

 | CONTENT-INV-10 | TestContentInv10_RedactionRemovesSecrets | Over generated secrets of every rule planted in HTML, JS, JSON and text, no secret survives in normalized snapshots, hunks, side-by-side rows, features, change sets or observations; Redact is idempotent.

 | CONTENT-INV-11 | TestContentInv11_ScoreImportsNoEgress | Scores lie in [0, 1] and equal the clamped sum of their reasons; reasons are unique, sorted, valid for oam; removals and returns alone score 0; score*.go imports nothing that reaches net, os or syscall.

 | CONTENT-INV-12 | TestContentInv12_Determinism | The same inputs give byte-identical processed captures, change sets and observations.

 | CONTENT-INV-13 | TestContentInv13_SniffByContent | Decisive bodies keep their kind under any media type; Mismatch is exactly "hint known and incompatible"; server-side source is a disclosure finding; Sniff is total and deterministic.

 | CONTENT-INV-14 | TestContentInv14_CoverageCompleteIff | Coverage is complete iff the outcome and body normalization are complete, every extractor that ran is complete and none applicable is canary-disabled.

 | CONTENT-INV-15 | TestContentInv15_Cause | Identical bodies with another normalizer version give cause normalizer, with another extractor set cause parser; changed bodies give target; the first complete capture is the baseline.

 | CONTENT-INV-16 | TestContentInv16_Canaries | Every extractor has exactly one passing canary; a disabled extractor's families are never covered.

 | CONTENT-INV-17 | TestContentInv17_DecodedBlobsRedacted | DecodeBlobs returns the text inside base64, base64>gzip and hex blobs with the outer span and encoding path, redacted for every secret rule; blobs inside secrets (JWT segments) are never decoded; binary payloads and plain text give nothing; the output is bounded by MaxResponseBytes and deterministic.

#### contentstore

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | CSTORE-INV-01 | TestCStoreInv01_IngestIdempotent | The same capture ingested again, sequentially or concurrently, is one snapshot: no new row, diff, finding, event, index document or quota count.

 | CSTORE-INV-02 | TestCStoreInv02_TenantIsolation | Identical content of two tenants is paid for by each (disjoint chunk namespaces, no dedupe benefit); no read, diff, pin, search hit or first-appeared hit crosses tenants; ids, keyed hashes and wrapped keys differ; one tenant's key cannot open the other's ciphertext.

 | CSTORE-INV-03 | TestCStoreInv03_RetentionKeepsPinnedAndNewest | Retention keeps exactly newest-KeepLast ∪ within-KeepFor ∪ pinned ∪ newest per key; diffs follow; survivors read back byte-exactly after keyframe rewrites; GC reclaims; stored-bytes usage equals the chunk bytes.

 | CSTORE-INV-04 | TestCStoreInv04_DiffDeterminism | Independent stores fed the same captures produce identical ids, manifests and change sets; Diff is byte-identical across calls; stored hunks and changes equal a recomputation.

 | CSTORE-INV-05 | TestCStoreInv05_RebuildEqualsIncremental | Inline, batched and rebuilt indexes hold the same documents under the same ids and answer a query set identically; a lost index is rebuilt from the watermark.

 | CSTORE-INV-06 | TestCStoreInv06_QuotaExact | Exactly N captures per day under a limit N (also concurrently), refusals store nothing, duplicates are free; stored-bytes limits admit iff usage + added ≤ limit; index-bytes limits refuse once reached.

 | CSTORE-INV-07 | TestCStoreInv07_EncryptionRoundTripNoPlaintext | For a secret of every named rule in a body and a header, findings decrypt to it, raw reads restore body and headers exactly, and no row, chunk object, decompressed record, decoded blob, diff record, index document or file, or outbox event holds it; tampering and other tenants' keys fail.

 | CSTORE-INV-08 | TestCStoreInv08_SPAStorageUnderQuarter | The SPA fixture's versions (raw snapshots: redacted bodies and capture records) take < 25 % of their raw size with the default codec; derived records are reported.

 | CSTORE-INV-09 | TestCStoreInv09_ValidityIntervals | Contiguous, disjoint [valid_from, valid_to) per key with one open version; unchanged re-fetches add no row; degraded captures are closed, empty and never open; as-of lookups return the containing version; unseen older captures are refused.

 | CSTORE-INV-10 | TestCStoreInv10_SearchDefaults | Hunk sides are separate documents; vendored bundles are excluded unless asked; decoded blobs are searchable in decoded only; first appeared returns the earliest adding snapshot, baselines included; query errors are VALIDATION_FAILED.

 | CSTORE-INV-11 | TestCStoreInv11_SPAEndToEnd | Renamed bundles link to their predecessors (lineage key, then SimHash) and an unrelated chunk does not; masked-only versions produce no documents; planted changes are findable by field query and regex; events carry a decodable observation and a valid patch; baselines are not alertable, the planted change is.

 | CSTORE-INV-12 | TestCStoreInv12_VolatilityLearned | A non-feature attribute changing on every capture of a stable page is frozen once (version 1), later captures use the learned normalizer and diff masked-only; feature paths are never counted.

 | CSTORE-INV-13 | TestCStoreInv13_ObservationReplay | The re-published observation of an unchanged re-fetch equals processing the capture afresh, byte for byte.

#### oam

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | OAM-INV-01 | TestOAMInv01_URLTemplateIdempotent | NormalizeURLTemplate is a fixed point on every accepted input.

 | OAM-INV-02 | TestOAMInv02_URLTemplateEquivalentSpellings | Equivalent spellings (case, default ports, IDN, escapes, dot segments, query/fragment, placeholder spellings, id-like literals) give the same template.

 | OAM-INV-03 | TestOAMInv03_URLTemplateConcreteHost | Every accepted template has a concrete, netnorm-canonical http(s) origin; placeholder-free URLs agree with netnorm.

 | OAM-INV-04 | TestOAMInv04_EndpointKey | Endpoint key = ep: + normalized method + + normalized template; Normalize is idempotent; source never changes the key.

 | OAM-INV-05 | TestOAMInv05_AuthoritativeConvergence (oam, graphstore, graphstore/pg) | With authoritative observations of every coverage, any permutation and duplication gives byte-identical state.

 | OAM-INV-06 | TestOAMInv06_LatestAuthoritativeNeverStale | An edge held by a unit's effective member set is active (covering runs included); one held by none and never reported classically is stale; held edges keep their targets active; header tags are visible iff held or classic.

 | OAM-INV-07 | TestOAMInv07_TagChangedExactlyOnce | Exactly one tag_changed per tag whose visible content changed per operation, none otherwise; its patch maps old to new.

 | OAM-INV-08 | TestOAMInv08_ChangeReplay | Folding the change log (base + patches) reproduces every entity's content, scope, state and tags and every edge's state and tags after every operation.

 | OAM-INV-09 | TestOAMInv09_ContentChangeIdempotent | RecordContentChange changes no state, is idempotent per (entity, from, to, diff), stores the normalized patch.

 | OAM-INV-10 | TestOAMInv10_CauseAndBaselineOnEvents | Every event of an observation carries its cause and baseline; covering-run events carry none.

 | OAM-INV-11 | TestOAMInv11_NoSecretValues | exposed_secret tags never contain the secret; LooksLikeSecret flags credentials, passes digests, UUIDs, previews and keyed hashes.

#### graphstore

 | Id | Test | Statement

 | OAM-INV-05 | TestOAMInv05_AuthoritativeConvergence (Mem, pg vs Mem) | Authoritative multisets in any order with duplicates converge, and pg equals Mem.

 | OAM-INV-12 | TestOAMInv12_AsOf (Mem, pg) | GetEntityAsOf(s) / NeighborsAsOf(s) equal the reference snapshot taken right after the operation that reached seq s, for every s of a random history; later entities are NOT_FOUND.

 | OAM-INV-13 | TestOAMInv13_MemEqualsPG (pg) | On random histories (classic and authoritative observations, re-deliveries, covering runs, content changes) pg and Mem have byte-identical canonical state and change logs (seq excluded).

 | OAM-INV-14 | TestOAMInv14_Commonness (Mem, pg) | Holders are distinct hosts of active sources over active edges; common iff holders > 50; withdrawn edges stop counting.

 | OAM-INV-15 | TestOAMInv15_KernelMatchesOAM (internal/kernel) | The kernel over the storage encoding reproduces oam.Graph (results, event ids, state) with authority, tags and covering runs.

## 40. Error codes

Every list is closed: a new code is a specification change. Codes are returned as values (Go: a typed code inside an error that unwraps to a package sentinel; Rust: a thiserror enum with the same names).

### 40.1 Parsers and codecs

 | Package | Codes

 | htmlparse | The 51 WHATWG tokenizer and input-stream parse-error names (abrupt-closing-of-empty-comment ... unknown-named-character-reference, including the processing-instruction errors disallowed-processing-instruction-target, eof-in-processing-instruction, invalid-first-character-of-processing-instruction-target, invalid-processing-instruction-target); Flowforge tree-construction names (the standard leaves them unnamed): missing-doctype, non-conforming-doctype, unexpected-doctype, unexpected-start-tag, unexpected-end-tag, unexpected-character, unclosed-element, eof-in-element, xmlns-attribute-mismatch; limits: nesting-depth-limit-exceeded, attribute-limit-exceeded, input-size-limit-exceeded

 | jsparse | syntax: unexpected-character, unterminated-string, unterminated-template, unterminated-regexp, unterminated-comment, invalid-escape, invalid-identifier-escape, invalid-number, numeric-separator, identifier-after-number, unexpected-token, unexpected-eof, expected-token, invalid-cover-grammar, newline-forbidden, mixed-coalesce, exponent-unary, module-item-position, escaped-keyword, for-in-of-head, invalid-optional-chain, invalid-assignment-target, invalid-pattern, regexp-flags, regexp-pattern, invalid-template-escape; early: duplicate-proto, redeclaration, reserved-word, strict-eval-arguments, strict-with, strict-delete, strict-octal, function-position, illegal-break, illegal-continue, duplicate-label, illegal-return, new-target, import-meta, super-call, super-property, yield-await-in-params, duplicate-param, non-simple-use-strict, private-name, class-constructor, static-prototype, class-field, static-block, let-name, export-name, accessor-params, labelled-function, invalid-keyword-use; limit: too-deep (Code.Class groups them as syntax, early and limit)

 | cssparse | eof_in_comment, eof_in_string, newline_in_string, eof_in_url, bad_url, eof_in_escape, invalid_escape, invalid_rule, invalid_declaration, eof_in_at_rule, eof_in_block, empty, extra_input, unexpected_close; limits depth_limit, input_too_large, limit_exceeded

 | xmlparse | unexpected_eof, no_root_element, invalid_utf8, invalid_char, unexpected_char, invalid_name, invalid_qname, text_outside_root, multiple_roots, unexpected_end_tag, mismatched_end_tag, duplicate_attribute, lt_in_attribute, invalid_reference, undefined_entity, invalid_char_ref, double_hyphen_in_comment, cdata_end_in_content, reserved_pi_target, invalid_xml_decl, misplaced_doctype, unbound_prefix, reserved_prefix, invalid_namespace_decl, unsupported_encoding, dtd_internal_subset, dtd_external_id, depth_limit, input_too_large

 | sourcemap | invalid_json, invalid_field, unsupported_version, missing_mappings, missing_sources, ambiguous_map, invalid_vlq_char, vlq_truncated, vlq_overflow, invalid_segment, index_out_of_range, negative_value, invalid_section_offset, section_order, section_overlap, nested_index_map, section_url, invalid_ignore_list, unsorted_mappings, input_too_large, limit_exceeded

 | wellknown | robots: invalid_line, rule_outside_group, invalid_user_agent, invalid_pattern, invalid_crawl_delay, invalid_utf8, input_too_large, limit_exceeded; security.txt: missing_contact, missing_expires, multiple_expires, invalid_expires, expired, expires_too_far (warning), insecure_uri, invalid_uri, multiple_preferred_languages, unknown_key, content_after_signature, unterminated_signature

 | codec | ErrInvalid: invalid_byte, bad_padding, trailing_bits, truncated, invalid_escape, non_canonical, line_too_long; ErrCorrupt: bad_header, corrupt; ErrOutputLimit: output_limit, ratio_limit; ErrUnsupported: unsupported. Offsets: bytes into the input for text codecs, output bytes returned for streams

 | dnsmsg | short_message, label_too_long, name_too_long, bad_label_type, pointer_out_of_bounds, pointer_loop, bad_rdata, rdlength_mismatch, trailing_data, bad_opt, bad_name_text, string_too_long, rdata_kind_mismatch, message_too_large, too_many_records

 | socks5 | short (with Need), bad_version, no_methods, empty_username, empty_password, bad_reserved, bad_command, bad_address_type, empty_domain, bad_reply_code, too_long, bad_address; handshakes: no_acceptable_method, auth_failed, command_not_supported, request_failed

The wellknown list above is the diagnostic vocabulary of conformance/wellknown/*.json, which is authoritative for exact names and levels.

### 40.2 Storage

 | Package | Codes

 | chunkstore | INVALID_CONFIG, INVALID_NAME, CLOSED, TOO_LARGE, BACKEND, OBJECT_NOT_FOUND, MANIFEST_NOT_FOUND, CHUNK_MISSING, PACK_MISSING, INDEX_MISSING, PACK_TRUNCATED, PACK_BAD_MAGIC, PACK_CHECKSUM, PACK_UNSUPPORTED, PACK_MALFORMED, RECORD_CHECKSUM, RECORD_MALFORMED, CHUNK_DECOMPRESS, CHUNK_LENGTH_MISMATCH, CHUNK_HASH_MISMATCH, INDEX_TRUNCATED, INDEX_BAD_MAGIC, INDEX_CHECKSUM, INDEX_UNSUPPORTED, INDEX_MALFORMED, INDEX_PACK_MISMATCH, MANIFEST_TRUNCATED, MANIFEST_BAD_MAGIC, MANIFEST_CHECKSUM, MANIFEST_UNSUPPORTED, MANIFEST_MALFORMED, MANIFEST_LENGTH_MISMATCH, REF_TRUNCATED, REF_BAD_MAGIC, REF_CHECKSUM, REF_UNSUPPORTED, REF_MALFORMED; fsck problems also ORPHAN_OBJECT (warning) and DUPLICATE_CHUNK. Each maps to a core.Kind

 | textindex (files) | ErrChecksum (a CRC), ErrCorrupt (structure), ErrSchemaMismatch, ErrDuplicateID

 | contentstore | NOT_FOUND, VALIDATION_FAILED, QUOTA_EXCEEDED (resource exhausted), CAPTURE_OUT_OF_ORDER (failed precondition), VERSION_CONFLICT (aborted; retried internally), CONTENT_INTEGRITY (internal), INTERNAL

 | graphstore | unchanged from Part I (11.8); content patches and AsOf arguments are INVALID_ARGUMENT, foreign entities NOT_FOUND

### 40.3 Query language

 | Code | When | Span

 | EMPTY_QUERY | no clause | whole query

 | QUERY_TOO_LONG | > 8,192 bytes | from byte 8,192

 | INVALID_UTF8 | invalid UTF-8 | first bad byte

 | UNEXPECTED_TOKEN | unmatched ), --x, junk after a group | the token

 | UNBALANCED_PAREN | ( never closed | ( to end

 | EXPECTED_TERM | missing operand after -, AND, OR, ( | the token found

 | EXPECTED_VALUE | field: not followed directly by a value | the position

 | UNTERMINATED_PHRASE, UNTERMINATED_REGEX | no closing quote or slash | opener to end

 | EMPTY_REGEX | // | the regex

 | BAD_REGEX_FLAG | flag other than i, m, s, or repeated | the flag

 | BAD_REGEX | RE2 syntax error or > 1 KiB | the regex

 | BAD_TIME | bad since/until value | the value

 | NESTING_TOO_DEEP, TOO_MANY_CLAUSES | limits | the offending token

 | UNKNOWN_FIELD | field not in the schema | the field name

 | FIELD_TYPE | regex on a keyword field, numeric or stored field, no time field | the clause

 | REGEX_NO_TRIGRAMS | regex without trigrams and the filters do not cap candidates | the regex

### 40.4 Egress refusal reasons (X-Flowforge-Refused)

unauthenticated, decision_missing, decision_unknown, decision_invalid, decision_not_allow, decision_expired, decision_run_mismatch, decision_log_unavailable, invalid_target, host_mismatch, port_not_allowed, no_pinned_ips, forbidden_address, redirect_denied, scope_check_failed, policy_unavailable, kill_switch, kill_switch_unknown, rate_limited, concurrency_limited, rate_invalid, intel_requires_passive, intel_unresolvable, dns_denied, dns_refused, tickets_exhausted, socks_protocol, command_not_supported, connect_failed, upstream_failed, notify_not_notifier, notify_https_only, notify_not_allowlisted, notify_no_resolver, notify_resolve_failed. Allowed intelligence-source connections are recorded with reason intel_source.

### 40.5 Alerts

A0202 (unknown field) on diff.score and diff.score_features: scores are not addressable by rules (36.5). Other codes as in 15.

## 41. Where the brief and the code differ

Each row is a place where docs/V2_DESIGN.md (§ numbers are the brief's) and the implementation disagreed. Part II follows the column "Specified"; the brief has been amended to match where noted.

 | Topic | Brief | Specified (code) | Why

 | Package table (brief §2) | Ten layer-1 packages, content, contentstore, services/content | Also codec, dnsmsg, socks5, xmlparse/sitemap, the lab; sitemaps live in xmlparse/sitemap, not wellknown | The DNS forwarder, SOCKS5 and bounded decoding needed their own state machines; layer-1 packages may not import each other

 | HTML and JS nesting bounds (§4.1.5, §12.10) | "configured limit"; §12.10 lists only XML/CSS depth 64 | HTML 512 open elements, JS 4000 syntactic nesting (26.4) | Real pages nest deeper than 64; recursive descent needs a bound; both are pinned constants

 | Sitemap limits (§4.3) | 50,000 URLs / 50 MB | Defaults 20,000 entries / 8 MiB, raisable to 50,000 / 50 MiB | §12.10's 8 MiB and 20,000-fact bounds win

 | HTML conformance target (§4.1) | 100 % | All except 6 scripted cases and 16 upstream tokenizer positions, asserted exactly | Scripting is off by design; the 16 upstream positions contradict the state machine

 | Snapshot DDL (§6.2) | columns of the original sketch; "migration in graph schema" | Schema content, migration 0031 (34.2): validity interval, signature, kind, outcome, coverage, degraded, simhash, pins, codec and delta base, seals, key id; programs, quota_*, index_state, data_keys, outbox tables | §12.12 storage rules, §6.4 envelope encryption and §11.18 quotas

 | Manifest encoding (§6.1) | "fixed binary record (or deterministic CBOR)" | Fixed binary FFMF (31.5); FFRF ref and FFZB delta frames added | One format, byte-identical across languages

 | Chunk acceptance (§6.1) | 50 versions in < 15 % of raw | Met: 1.55 % (chunk store), 4.70 % with the content store's delta codec | —

 | Storage acceptance (§10.7) | all versions < 25 % of raw | Measured on raw snapshots (capture record + redacted body): 6.8 % in the lab; all records including diffs 18.4 % | On tiny fixtures fixed per-version records dominate (G-CST-05)

 | Coverage and diff fallback (§3.4) | "no diff fallback" is a condition of completeness | Coverage is decided per capture before any diff; a fallback does not affect it (28.4) | Coverage must not depend on the predecessor; a hash-only diff still has a complete reading

 | AsOf (§3.4) | replay onto the nearest snapshot | replay from the empty graph (28.7) | Same results; a snapshot start is an optimisation

 | extract, diff_latest emissions (§8) | emit Endpoint, File, edges, content_changed | read only; the content service's event is the single writer (36.6) | A second writer would apply authority twice

 | Raw tool output (§6.3) | raw_ref moves into the chunk store | Content tools store the raw response as the raw snapshot; other tools' raw_ref raw protocol capture is tracked by G-SVC-16 | —

 | REST search route (ROADMAP_V2 D5) | GET /v1/programs/{pid}/changes/search | GET /v1/changes/search?program_id= (37.2) | Matches the other content routes

 | Change-event types (ROADMAP_V2 D4) | endpoint_added, endpoint_removed, header_changed, secret_suspected as graph event types | content_changed and tag_changed only; level-3 kinds are content-change kinds (diff.kinds, kind: in search) | The brief (§3.4) superseded the roadmap

 | Alert diff fields (ROADMAP_V2 D4) | diff.kind, diff.added, diff.removed, diff.url, diff.feature | 36.5 fields; no score | §12.4 scores never decide

 | Lineage threshold | SimHash ≤ 6, ties by size ratio | Same, after a unique lineage-key match; candidates also include the host's open versions (34.1) | Lazily loaded chunks have no page reference

 | Catalog class, re-derive, KMS adapter, hybrid search | specified as v2 features | Catalog and the KMS adapter are v3; re-derive is specified here and in progress (G-CST-04); hybrid ships only after the labelled-set comparison and is not enabled | D4 defers advisories; the KEK port is in place

 | Quotas (§11.18) | per-tenant limits, unquantified | three limits per tenant, zero = unlimited, no defaults (34.6) | Pricing decides values, not the code

 | Score weights (§12.4) | named kinds, unquantified | The table of 33.11 | Pinned so ranks are reproducible

Flowforge specification, version 0.4 draft (Part II: v2). Graph model aligned to the OWASP Amass Open Asset Model; see the Open Asset Model and asset-db documentation for the upstream definitions.
